# domain/29DoF/                                                         

===== domain/29DoF/pd_humanoid.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Disambiguation test #2: does a hand-designed PD balance controller (no
CEM, no learning at all) also plateau near ~100 steps on Humanoid-v5, or
does it do much better? If even a hand-tuned classical controller
struggles similarly, that confirms the bottleneck is 3D balance itself
(a task property), not something specific to CEM/rank-k search (a
method property) - the same disambiguation Walker2d's tune_balance.py
provided for the 2D case, now run on the 3D body.

Simple PD toward an upright pose, with a tilt-correction term using the
quaternion's x/y components as small-angle roll/pitch proxies (qpos[4]
~roll-ish, qpos[5]~pitch-ish for small tilts from upright [1,0,0,0]).
"""
import sys
import numpy as np
import gymnasium as gym

# joint order: abdomen_z,y,x | r_hip_x,z,y | r_knee | l_hip_x,z,y | l_knee | arms...
KP = np.array([2,2,2,  3,2,3, 3,  3,2,3, 3,  1,1,1, 1,1,1], dtype=float)
KD = np.array([0.3]*17)
TARGET = np.zeros(17)
TARGET[6] = -0.3   # right knee - slight bend
TARGET[10] = -0.3  # left knee - slight bend
BAL_KP, BAL_KD = 8.0, 1.5


def controller(data):
    qpos_j = data.qpos[7:24]
    qvel_j = data.qvel[6:23]
    roll_proxy = data.qpos[4]
    pitch_proxy = data.qpos[5]
    roll_rate = data.qvel[4]
    pitch_rate = data.qvel[5]

    target = TARGET.copy()
    pitch_corr = -BAL_KP * pitch_proxy - BAL_KD * pitch_rate
    roll_corr = -BAL_KP * roll_proxy - BAL_KD * roll_rate
    # hip_y (index 2 of each leg's 3) controls fwd/back; hip_x (index 0) controls side
    target[2] += pitch_corr   # right hip_y
    target[8] += pitch_corr   # left hip_y
    target[0] += roll_corr    # right hip_x
    target[6-6+0] = target[0]  # (no-op, keeping clarity)
    target[7] += roll_corr    # left hip_x

    torque = KP * (target - qpos_j) - KD * qvel_j
    return np.clip(torque, -0.4, 0.4)


def rollout(env, seed, max_steps):
    obs, info = env.reset(seed=seed)
    data = env.unwrapped.data
    steps = 0
    for t in range(max_steps):
        act = controller(data)
        obs, r, term, trunc, info = env.step(act)
        steps += 1
        if term:
            break
    return steps


if __name__ == "__main__":
    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    seeds = list(range(100, 120))
    results = [rollout(env, s, 1000) for s in seeds]
    env.close()
    print(f"Hand-designed PD balance controller, Humanoid-v5, 20 held-out seeds:")
    print(f"  mean = {np.mean(results):.1f}  individual = {results}")
    print(f"  for reference: random=26.1, flat-linear-CEM=97.2, rank4-CEM=112.7")

===== domain/29DoF/rank4_humanoid_legsonly.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Disambiguation test: rank4_humanoid.py plateaued around 100-170/1000 steps
even with 3x the search budget - a genuine plateau, not a budget problem.
Two possible explanations: (a) still too many joints/DoF for this search
budget, or (b) Humanoid-v5's TRUE 3D balance is just a harder problem
than Walker2d's 2D-constrained one, independent of joint count (arms
don't touch the ground and barely affect falling dynamics either way).

This test freezes the 6 arm joints (both shoulders x2, both elbows),
leaving 11 active joints (abdomen x3, hips x3 x2, knees x2) - closer to
the doc's "legs-only, no arms" intermediate. If THIS also plateaus near
the 17-DoF result, that's evidence for (b): the bottleneck is 3D balance
itself, not joint count, and a true fix needs a genuinely 2D-constrained
or more-capable architecture, not just fewer DoF.
"""
import sys, os, time
import numpy as np
import gymnasium as gym

# Humanoid-v5 joint order: abdomen_z,y,x(0-2), r_hip_x,z,y(3-5), r_knee(6),
# l_hip_x,z,y(7-9), l_knee(10), r_shoulder1,2(11-12), r_elbow(13),
# l_shoulder1,2(14-15), l_elbow(16)
ACTIVE_IDX = list(range(0, 11))   # abdomen + both legs
FROZEN_IDX = list(range(11, 17))  # both arms

ACT_DIM = len(ACTIVE_IDX)  # 11
FEAT_DIM = 1 + 4 + 17 + 3 + 17  # same full feature set (42) - sees everything,
                                 # just doesn't ACT on the arms
RANK = 4
DIM = RANK * FEAT_DIM + ACT_DIM * RANK + ACT_DIM  # 168 + 44 + 11 = 223


def get_features(data):
    return np.concatenate([
        [data.qpos[2]], data.qpos[3:7], data.qpos[7:24],
        data.qvel[3:6], data.qvel[6:23],
    ])


def unpack(theta):
    i = RANK * FEAT_DIM
    j = i + ACT_DIM * RANK
    V = theta[:i].reshape(RANK, FEAT_DIM)
    U = theta[i:j].reshape(ACT_DIM, RANK)
    b = theta[j:]
    return V, U, b


def policy_action(theta, feat):
    V, U, b = unpack(theta)
    z = V @ feat
    active = 0.4 * np.tanh(U @ z + b)
    full = np.zeros(17)
    full[ACTIVE_IDX] = active
    return full  # frozen joints get 0 torque


def rollout(theta, env, seed, max_steps):
    obs, info = env.reset(seed=seed)
    data = env.unwrapped.data
    steps = 0
    for t in range(max_steps):
        feat = get_features(data)
        act = policy_action(theta, feat)
        obs, r, term, trunc, info = env.step(act)
        steps += 1
        if term:
            break
    return steps


def fitness(theta, env, seeds, max_steps):
    return np.mean([rollout(theta, env, s, max_steps) for s in seeds])


def cem(pop_size=80, generations=30, max_steps=400, n_eval_seeds=3, seed=0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    mean = np.zeros(DIM)
    std = np.ones(DIM) * 0.3
    n_elite = max(1, int(pop_size * 0.2))
    for gen in range(generations):
        pop = mean + std * rng.standard_normal((pop_size, DIM))
        gen_seeds = rng.integers(0, 100_000, size=n_eval_seeds).tolist()
        fits = np.array([fitness(theta, env, gen_seeds, max_steps) for theta in pop])
        elite_idx = np.argsort(fits)[-n_elite:]
        elite = pop[elite_idx]
        mean = elite.mean(axis=0)
        std = elite.std(axis=0) + 1e-3
        print(f"  gen {gen:2d}  pop_mean={fits.mean():7.1f}  pop_best={fits.max():7.1f}  "
              f"std_norm={np.linalg.norm(std):.3f}", flush=True)
    env.close()
    return mean


if __name__ == "__main__":
    gens = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 30
    print(f"Rank-4, ARMS FROZEN (11 active DoF, DIM={DIM} params), "
          f"pop=80, gens={gens}, max_steps=400")
    t0 = time.time()
    best_theta = cem(generations=gens)
    print(f"\nCEM finished in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s", flush=True)

    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    held_out_perf = fitness(best_theta, env, list(range(100, 120)), max_steps=1000)
    env.close()
    print(f"legs-only rank-4 policy, held-out (20 seeds), cap 1000: {held_out_perf:.1f} steps")
    print(f"full-17-DoF rank-4 reference (90 gens): 112.7 steps")
    print(f"full-17-DoF flat-linear reference (30 gens): 97.2 steps")
    print(f"random baseline: 26.1 steps")
    np.save("rank4_humanoid_legsonly_theta.npy", best_theta)

===== domain/29DoF/rank4_humanoid.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
The natural next test after rank4_walker.py's clean pass (896.2 vs 920.9,
97.3% match) and cem_humanoid.py's confirmation that the dimensionality
wall is real at 731 flat params on Humanoid-v5 (97.2/1000, barely above
random's 26.1 - the SAME budget that nearly solves Walker2d). Does a
rank-4 head recover what the flat policy lost, on Humanoid, the same
way it matched linear on Walker2d?

a = 0.4 * tanh(U @ (V @ phi(s)) + b)
V: (4, 42), U: (17, 4), b: (17,) -> 168 + 68 + 17 = 253 params
(close to REALISTIC_CLAIMS_NEXTSTEPS_NOV2nd.md's own estimate, "~265")

Same features, same fitness (survival steps), same budget (pop=80,
gens=30, max_steps=400) as cem_humanoid.py, for a clean before/after.
"""
import sys, os, time
import numpy as np
import gymnasium as gym

ACT_DIM = 17
FEAT_DIM = 42
RANK = 4
DIM = RANK * FEAT_DIM + ACT_DIM * RANK + ACT_DIM  # 253


def get_features(data):
    return np.concatenate([
        [data.qpos[2]],
        data.qpos[3:7],
        data.qpos[7:24],
        data.qvel[3:6],
        data.qvel[6:23],
    ])


def unpack(theta):
    i = RANK * FEAT_DIM
    j = i + ACT_DIM * RANK
    V = theta[:i].reshape(RANK, FEAT_DIM)
    U = theta[i:j].reshape(ACT_DIM, RANK)
    b = theta[j:]
    return V, U, b


def policy_action(theta, feat):
    V, U, b = unpack(theta)
    z = V @ feat
    return 0.4 * np.tanh(U @ z + b)


def rollout(theta, env, seed, max_steps):
    obs, info = env.reset(seed=seed)
    data = env.unwrapped.data
    steps = 0
    for t in range(max_steps):
        feat = get_features(data)
        act = policy_action(theta, feat)
        obs, r, term, trunc, info = env.step(act)
        steps += 1
        if term:
            break
    return steps


def fitness(theta, env, seeds, max_steps):
    return np.mean([rollout(theta, env, s, max_steps) for s in seeds])


def cem(pop_size=80, generations=30, max_steps=400, n_eval_seeds=3, seed=0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    mean = np.zeros(DIM)
    std = np.ones(DIM) * 0.3
    n_elite = max(1, int(pop_size * 0.2))
    for gen in range(generations):
        pop = mean + std * rng.standard_normal((pop_size, DIM))
        gen_seeds = rng.integers(0, 100_000, size=n_eval_seeds).tolist()
        fits = np.array([fitness(theta, env, gen_seeds, max_steps) for theta in pop])
        elite_idx = np.argsort(fits)[-n_elite:]
        elite = pop[elite_idx]
        mean = elite.mean(axis=0)
        std = elite.std(axis=0) + 1e-3
        print(f"  gen {gen:2d}  pop_mean={fits.mean():7.1f}  pop_best={fits.max():7.1f}  "
              f"std_norm={np.linalg.norm(std):.3f}", flush=True)
    env.close()
    return mean


if __name__ == "__main__":
    gens = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 30
    print(f"Rank-4 head on Humanoid-v5: DIM={DIM} params (vs flat linear's 731), "
          f"pop=80, gens={gens}, max_steps=400")
    t0 = time.time()
    best_theta = cem(generations=gens)
    print(f"\nCEM finished in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s", flush=True)

    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    held_out_perf = fitness(best_theta, env, list(range(100, 120)), max_steps=1000)
    env.close()
    print(f"rank-4 policy, held-out (20 seeds), cap 1000: {held_out_perf:.1f} steps")
    print(f"flat-linear reference (cem_humanoid.py, same budget): 97.2 steps")
    print(f"random baseline (same seeds): 26.1 steps")
    np.save("rank4_humanoid_theta.npy", best_theta)

===== domain/29DoF/rank4_walker.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
REALISTIC_CLAIMS_NEXTSTEPS_NOV2nd.md's own load-bearing experiment, run
before any 29-DoF attempt: does a rank-4 head match the proven 96-param
linear policy on Walker2d? "If rank-4 fails on 6 DoF, the scaling
hypothesis is already broken and no amount of G1 work will fix it."

Same everything as mw_to_protomotions/cem_policy_search.py (features,
fitness=survival steps, CEM mechanics) and the SAME pop/gens/max_steps
that produced the proven 920.9-step linear-policy result
(domain/PLAN_A_PASSED.md: pop=80, gens=30, max_steps=400) - only the
policy parameterisation changes:

  linear:  a = tanh(W @ phi(s) + b),        W: (6,15)          -> 96 params
  rank-4:  a = tanh(U @ (V @ phi(s)) + b),  V: (4,15), U: (6,4) -> 90 params

Direct, apples-to-apples comparison, not a re-estimate.
"""
import sys, os, time
import numpy as np
import gymnasium as gym

ACT_DIM = 6
FEAT_DIM = 15
RANK = 4
DIM = RANK * FEAT_DIM + ACT_DIM * RANK + ACT_DIM  # 60 + 24 + 6 = 90


def get_features(data):
    return np.concatenate([
        [data.qpos[2], data.qvel[2], data.qpos[1]],
        data.qpos[3:9],
        data.qvel[3:9],
    ])


def unpack(theta):
    i = RANK * FEAT_DIM
    j = i + ACT_DIM * RANK
    V = theta[:i].reshape(RANK, FEAT_DIM)
    U = theta[i:j].reshape(ACT_DIM, RANK)
    b = theta[j:]
    return V, U, b


def policy_action(theta, feat):
    V, U, b = unpack(theta)
    z = V @ feat
    return np.tanh(U @ z + b)


def rollout(theta, seed, max_steps):
    env = gym.make("Walker2d-v5", exclude_current_positions_from_observation=False)
    obs, info = env.reset(seed=seed)
    data = env.unwrapped.data
    steps = 0
    for t in range(max_steps):
        feat = get_features(data)
        act = policy_action(theta, feat)
        obs, r, term, trunc, info = env.step(act)
        steps += 1
        if term:
            break
    env.close()
    return steps


def fitness(theta, seeds, max_steps):
    return np.mean([rollout(theta, s, max_steps) for s in seeds])


def cem(pop_size=80, elite_frac=0.2, generations=30, max_steps=400,
        eval_seeds=(0, 1), seed=0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    mean = np.zeros(DIM)
    std = np.ones(DIM) * 0.5
    n_elite = max(1, int(pop_size * elite_frac))
    for gen in range(generations):
        pop = mean + std * rng.standard_normal((pop_size, DIM))
        fits = np.array([fitness(theta, eval_seeds, max_steps) for theta in pop])
        elite_idx = np.argsort(fits)[-n_elite:]
        elite = pop[elite_idx]
        mean = elite.mean(axis=0)
        std = elite.std(axis=0) + 1e-3
        print(f"  gen {gen:2d}  pop_mean={fits.mean():6.1f}  pop_best={fits.max():6.1f}  "
              f"std_norm={np.linalg.norm(std):.3f}", flush=True)
    return mean


if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(f"Rank-4 head on Walker2d: DIM={DIM} params (vs linear's 96), "
          f"pop=80, gens=30, max_steps=400 - identical budget to the proven "
          f"linear-policy run (920.9 steps held-out)")
    t0 = time.time()
    best_theta = cem()
    train_time = time.time() - t0
    print(f"\nCEM search finished in {train_time:.1f}s", flush=True)

    held_out_seeds = list(range(100, 120))
    rank4_perf = fitness(best_theta, held_out_seeds, max_steps=1000)
    print(f"rank-4 policy, held-out seeds (20), cap 1000 steps: {rank4_perf:.1f} steps mean")
    print(f"linear-policy reference (same held-out protocol, PLAN_A_PASSED.md): 920.9 steps")

    rng = np.random.default_rng(999)
    random_theta = rng.standard_normal(DIM) * 0.5
    random_perf = fitness(random_theta, held_out_seeds, max_steps=1000)
    print(f"untrained random policy, same held-out seeds: {random_perf:.1f} steps")
    np.save("rank4_walker_theta.npy", best_theta)


# domain/                                                               

===== domain/b1_runtime.py =====
"""
mw_to_protomotions/b1_runtime
==============================
B1: posterior action sample. The cheapest Plan B runtime — one control-step
query per step. No learned policy, no CEM over policy parameters. Just:

  1. Sample candidate actions from the local action posterior
  2. Filter by MW distance to the transition manifold
  3. Rank by dense task reward
  4. Execute best

Cost per step: ~30ms on CPU (dominated by the MW distance eval over the
candidate set). Fits a 50Hz control loop with headroom.
"""
from __future__ import annotations
import numpy as np
import torch
from mw_framework.query.inverse import inverse_query, _build_query_batch
from mw_framework.domains.transition import (
    STATE_FEATURES, ACTION_FEATURES, A_MAX)


def _dense_reward(s_row: dict, a: np.ndarray, sp_row: dict,
                  goal: dict, lam_ctrl: float = 0.01,
                  lam_mw: float = 0.1, det=None) -> float:
    """Dense reward — no sparse indicator, no +1 on success. Must be
    differentiable-ish in the action for the ranking to mean anything.

    R = -||sp_forward_vel - goal.forward_vel||^2
        - lam_ctrl * ||a||^2
        - lam_mw * MW_distance(s, a, sp)
    """
    # forward velocity proxy: height change is not forward velocity;
    # use the state's pitch_rate and thigh motion as a proxy for progress.
    # For a real system, read sp.forward_vel from the state schema.
    progress = -abs(sp_row.get("pitch_rate", 0.0) - goal.get("forward_vel", 0.0))
    effort = lam_ctrl * float(np.sum(a ** 2))
    if det is not None:
        a_t = torch.as_tensor(a[None], dtype=torch.float32)
        xb = _build_query_batch(det, s_row, a_t, sp_row)
        with torch.no_grad():
            mw, _ = det.vae_.mw_distance(xb, mc_samples=2)
        mw_pen = lam_mw * float(mw.mean().item())
    else:
        mw_pen = 0.0
    return float(progress) - effort - mw_pen


def b1_step(det, s_row: dict, goal: dict, sp_prior: dict,
            n_candidates: int = 64, seed: int | None = None) -> np.ndarray:
    """One control step: sample candidate actions, rank by reward, return best.

    sp_prior: the expected next state (e.g. from a short PD integration, or
              simply the current state — the MW filter will reject the
              action if the resulting transition is off-manifold).
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    m = len(ACTION_FEATURES)

    # 1. Sample candidates: a local Gaussian around the median *learned*
    #    action (from the last inverse_query) plus a random subset to
    #    avoid collapse to a single mode.
    init_a = inverse_query(det, s_row, sp_prior, n_pop=16, n_gen=2, seed=seed)
    a_local = init_a + 0.15 * rng.standard_normal((n_candidates // 2, m))
    a_random = rng.uniform(-A_MAX, A_MAX, (n_candidates // 2, m))
    candidates = np.clip(np.concatenate([a_local, a_random], axis=0),
                         -A_MAX, A_MAX)

    # 2. Score each candidate
    scores = [_dense_reward(s_row, a, sp_prior, goal, det=det)
              for a in candidates]
    best = candidates[int(np.argmax(scores))]
    return best

===== domain/cem_humanoid.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Direct test of EXPLANATION_OF_WALKER_FOR_AGENT.md's Gap 4 warning ("the
dimensionality wall is real... do not attempt a 29-DoF humanoid with the
current linear policy") - but at Humanoid-v5's actual 17 DoF, a real
middle ground between Walker2d (96 params, proven) and the full 29-DoF
target the doc warns about. Same CEM recipe as run_plan_a.sh
(cem_policy_search.py), same crawl-before-walk order: survival/balance
fitness first (the easier sub-problem), same as Walker2d's own history.

Policy: a = 0.4 * tanh(W @ phi(s) + b). Humanoid-v5's actuator range is
[-0.4, 0.4], not [-1, 1] like Walker2d - scaled accordingly.

Features (42 dims): height, 4-dim root quaternion, 17 joint angles,
3-dim root angular velocity, 17 joint velocities.
"""
import sys, os, time
import numpy as np
import gymnasium as gym

ACT_DIM = 17
FEAT_DIM = 1 + 4 + 17 + 3 + 17   # 42
DIM = ACT_DIM * FEAT_DIM + ACT_DIM  # 731


def get_features(data):
    return np.concatenate([
        [data.qpos[2]],          # height
        data.qpos[3:7],          # root quaternion
        data.qpos[7:24],         # 17 joint angles
        data.qvel[3:6],          # root angular velocity
        data.qvel[6:23],         # 17 joint velocities
    ])


def unpack(theta):
    W = theta[:ACT_DIM * FEAT_DIM].reshape(ACT_DIM, FEAT_DIM)
    b = theta[ACT_DIM * FEAT_DIM:]
    return W, b


def policy_action(theta, feat):
    W, b = unpack(theta)
    return 0.4 * np.tanh(W @ feat + b)


def rollout(theta, env, seed, max_steps):
    obs, info = env.reset(seed=seed)
    data = env.unwrapped.data
    steps = 0
    for t in range(max_steps):
        feat = get_features(data)
        act = policy_action(theta, feat)
        obs, r, term, trunc, info = env.step(act)
        steps += 1
        if term:
            break
    return steps


def fitness(theta, env, seeds, max_steps):
    return np.mean([rollout(theta, env, s, max_steps) for s in seeds])


def cem(pop_size, generations, max_steps, n_eval_seeds, seed=0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    mean = np.zeros(DIM)
    std = np.ones(DIM) * 0.3
    n_elite = max(1, int(pop_size * 0.2))
    for gen in range(generations):
        pop = mean + std * rng.standard_normal((pop_size, DIM))
        gen_seeds = rng.integers(0, 100_000, size=n_eval_seeds).tolist()
        fits = np.array([fitness(theta, env, gen_seeds, max_steps) for theta in pop])
        elite_idx = np.argsort(fits)[-n_elite:]
        elite = pop[elite_idx]
        mean = elite.mean(axis=0)
        std = elite.std(axis=0) + 1e-3
        print(f"  gen {gen:2d}  pop_mean={fits.mean():7.1f}  pop_best={fits.max():7.1f}  "
              f"std_norm={np.linalg.norm(std):.3f}")
    env.close()
    return mean


if __name__ == "__main__":
    pop = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 80
    gens = int(sys.argv[2]) if len(sys.argv) > 2 else 20
    max_steps = int(sys.argv[3]) if len(sys.argv) > 3 else 400
    print(f"CEM on Humanoid-v5: DIM={DIM} params ({DIM/96:.1f}x Walker2d's 96), "
          f"pop={pop}, gens={gens}, max_steps={max_steps}, fitness=survival steps")
    t0 = time.time()
    best_theta = cem(pop_size=pop, generations=gens, max_steps=max_steps, n_eval_seeds=3)
    print(f"\nCEM finished in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s")

    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    held_out_perf = fitness(best_theta, env, list(range(100, 120)), max_steps=1000)
    env.close()
    print(f"held-out mean survival (20 seeds, cap 1000): {held_out_perf:.1f} steps")

    rng = np.random.default_rng(999)
    random_theta = rng.standard_normal(DIM) * 0.3
    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    random_perf = fitness(random_theta, env, list(range(100, 120)), max_steps=1000)
    env.close()
    print(f"untrained random policy, same seeds: {random_perf:.1f} steps")
    np.save("cem_humanoid_theta.npy", best_theta)

===== domain/exp_e1_pd_fixed.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
E1 - Fixed joint indices for the hand-designed PD balance controller.
Sweeps pitch-sign and roll-sign, and a small (kp, kd) grid, because the
joint-axis sign cannot be assumed from the URDF alone.
"""
import sys
import numpy as np
import gymnasium as gym

KP_STATIC = np.array([2,2,2,  3,2,3, 3,  3,2,3, 3,  1,1,1, 1,1,1], dtype=float)
KD = np.array([0.3] * 17)
TARGET = np.zeros(17)
TARGET[6] = -0.3
TARGET[10] = -0.3

# Correct joint indices (abdomen_z,y,x | r_hip_x,z,y | r_knee | l_hip_x,z,y | l_knee | arms)
PITCH_IDX = [1, 5, 9]    # abdomen_y, right_hip_y, left_hip_y
ROLL_IDX  = [2, 3, 7]    # abdomen_x, right_hip_x, left_hip_x


def controller(data, pitch_sign, roll_sign, bal_kp, bal_kd):
    qpos_j = data.qpos[7:24]
    qvel_j = data.qvel[6:23]

    roll_proxy  = data.qpos[4]
    pitch_proxy = data.qpos[5]
    roll_rate   = data.qvel[4]
    pitch_rate  = data.qvel[5]

    target = TARGET.copy()
    pitch_corr = pitch_sign * (-bal_kp * pitch_proxy - bal_kd * pitch_rate)
    roll_corr  = roll_sign  * (-bal_kp * roll_proxy  - bal_kd * roll_rate)

    for i in PITCH_IDX:
        target[i] += pitch_corr
    for i in ROLL_IDX:
        target[i] += roll_corr

    torque = KP_STATIC * (target - qpos_j) - KD * qvel_j
    return np.clip(torque, -0.4, 0.4)


def rollout(pitch_sign, roll_sign, bal_kp, bal_kd, seed, max_steps=1000):
    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    obs, info = env.reset(seed=seed)
    data = env.unwrapped.data
    steps = 0
    for _ in range(max_steps):
        act = controller(data, pitch_sign, roll_sign, bal_kp, bal_kd)
        obs, r, term, trunc, info = env.step(act)
        steps += 1
        if term:
            break
    env.close()
    return steps


def sweep(pitch_signs, roll_signs, kp_vals, kd_vals, n_held_out=10):
    results = []
    held_out_seeds = list(range(100, 100 + n_held_out))
    for ps in pitch_signs:
        for rs in roll_signs:
            for kp in kp_vals:
                for kd in kd_vals:
                    perfs = [rollout(ps, rs, kp, kd, s) for s in held_out_seeds]
                    mean = float(np.mean(perfs))
                    results.append((ps, rs, kp, kd, mean, perfs))
                    print(f"  pitch_sign={ps:+d}  roll_sign={rs:+d}  "
                          f"kp={kp:>4.1f}  kd={kd:>4.1f}  "
                          f"mean={mean:6.1f}  "
                          f"best={max(perfs):>4d}  worst={min(perfs):>3d}",
                          flush=True)
    return results


if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("E1 - Fixed PD controller, Humanoid-v5")
    print("Reference (buggy PD from PROGRESS_LOG.md): 58.1 steps")
    print("Reference (rank-4 CEM, best so far):      112.7 steps")
    print("Reference (Walker2d linear, for scale):   920.9 steps")
    print()

    results = sweep(
        pitch_signs=[+1, -1],
        roll_signs=[+1, -1],
        kp_vals=[4.0, 8.0, 12.0],
        kd_vals=[0.8, 1.5, 2.5],
        n_held_out=10,
    )
    results.sort(key=lambda r: -r[4])
    print(f"\nTop 5 configurations:")
    for ps, rs, kp, kd, mean, perfs in results[:5]:
        print(f"  pitch_sign={ps:+d}  roll_sign={rs:+d}  "
              f"kp={kp:>4.1f}  kd={kd:>4.1f}  mean={mean:6.1f}")

    best = results[0]
    print(f"\nBest mean: {best[4]:.1f} steps")
    print(f"  vs buggy PD:              58.1  ({best[4] / 58.1:>5.2f}x)")
    print(f"  vs rank-4 CEM (112.7):    {best[4] / 112.7:>5.2f}x")
    print(f"  vs Walker2d (920.9):      {best[4] / 920.9:>5.2f}x")

    np.save("exp_e1_pd_best_config.npy",
            np.array([best[0], best[1], best[2], best[3]], dtype=float))
    print(f"\nSaved best config to exp_e1_pd_best_config.npy")

===== domain/exp_e1_vae_policy_compress.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
EXP_03 / E1 — Train a conditional VAE on (s, a) pairs from the proven
920-step balance policy. Measure whether the VAE can act as that policy
from state alone.

Loads cem_best_theta.npy, generates training + held-out data, trains a
CVAE, evaluates:
  1. Reconstruction error (does the VAE learn (s,a)?)
  2. Policy replacement error (can it act from s alone?)
  3. Rollout survival (is it functionally equivalent to the original?)
  4. Stochastic vs deterministic inference (does sample() help or hurt?)
"""
import sys, os, time
sys.path.insert(0, os.getcwd())
import numpy as np
import gymnasium as gym
import torch
torch.set_num_threads(8)
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F
import torch.optim as optim

# ── data collection ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def collect(theta, seeds, max_steps=1000):
    """Runs the linear policy in Walker2d, returns (S, A) arrays."""
    from mw_to_protomotions.cem_policy_search import get_features, policy_action
    S, A = [], []
    for seed in seeds:
        env = gym.make("Walker2d-v5", exclude_current_positions_from_observation=False)
        obs, info = env.reset(seed=seed)
        data = env.unwrapped.data
        for _ in range(max_steps):
            feat = get_features(data)
            act = policy_action(theta, feat)
            S.append(feat); A.append(act)
            obs, r, term, trunc, info = env.step(act)
            if term:
                break
        env.close()
    return np.array(S, dtype=np.float32), np.array(A, dtype=np.float32)


# ── CVAE ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
class CVAE(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, state_dim=15, action_dim=6, latent_dim=8, hidden=64):
        super().__init__()
        self.state_dim, self.action_dim, self.latent_dim = state_dim, action_dim, latent_dim

        # q(z | s, a)
        self.enc = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(state_dim + action_dim, hidden), nn.SiLU(),
            nn.Linear(hidden, hidden), nn.SiLU(),
        )
        self.enc_mu  = nn.Linear(hidden, latent_dim)
        self.enc_sig = nn.Linear(hidden, latent_dim)

        # p(z | s)
        self.prior = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(state_dim, hidden), nn.SiLU(),
            nn.Linear(hidden, hidden), nn.SiLU(),
        )
        self.prior_mu  = nn.Linear(hidden, latent_dim)
        self.prior_sig = nn.Linear(hidden, latent_dim)

        # p(a | z, s)
        self.dec = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(latent_dim + state_dim, hidden), nn.SiLU(),
            nn.Linear(hidden, hidden), nn.SiLU(),
            nn.Linear(hidden, action_dim), nn.Tanh(),
        )

    def encode(self, s, a):
        h = self.enc(torch.cat([s, a], -1))
        return self.enc_mu(h), F.softplus(self.enc_sig(h)) + 1e-3

    def prior_params(self, s):
        h = self.prior(s)
        return self.prior_mu(h), F.softplus(self.prior_sig(h)) + 1e-3

    def decode(self, z, s):
        return self.dec(torch.cat([z, s], -1))

    def forward(self, s, a, beta=0.01):
        mu_q, sig_q = self.encode(s, a)
        z = mu_q + sig_q * torch.randn_like(sig_q)
        a_hat = self.decode(z, s)
        recon = ((a - a_hat) ** 2).sum(-1).mean()

        mu_p, sig_p = self.prior_params(s)
        kl = 0.5 * (sig_q**2/sig_p**2 + (mu_q - mu_p)**2/sig_p**2
                    - 2*torch.log(sig_q/sig_p) - 1).sum(-1).mean()

        return recon + beta * kl, recon, kl

    @torch.no_grad()
    def act_deterministic(self, s):
        mu_p, _ = self.prior_params(s)
        return self.decode(mu_p, s)

    @torch.no_grad()
    def act_stochastic(self, s):
        mu_p, sig_p = self.prior_params(s)
        z = mu_p + sig_p * torch.randn_like(sig_p)
        return self.decode(z, s)


# ── training ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def train_cvae(S, A, latent_dim=8, hidden=64, beta=0.01, epochs=200,
               batch_size=512, lr=1e-3):
    S_t = torch.as_tensor(S); A_t = torch.as_tensor(A)
    model = CVAE(S.shape[1], A.shape[1], latent_dim, hidden)
    opt = optim.Adam(model.parameters(), lr=lr)
    n = len(S)
    for ep in range(epochs):
        perm = torch.randperm(n)
        tot, tot_recon, tot_kl = 0.0, 0.0, 0.0
        for start in range(0, n, batch_size):
            idx = perm[start:start+batch_size]
            loss, recon, kl = model(S_t[idx], A_t[idx], beta)
            opt.zero_grad(); loss.backward(); opt.step()
            tot += loss.item(); tot_recon += recon.item(); tot_kl += kl.item()
        if (ep+1) % 50 == 0 or ep == 0:
            print(f"    ep {ep+1:>3}/{epochs}  loss={tot:.3f}  "
                  f"recon={tot_recon:.4f}  kl={tot_kl:.3f}", flush=True)
    model.eval()
    return model


# ── evaluation ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def eval_reconstruction(model, S, A):
    with torch.no_grad():
        S_t = torch.as_tensor(S); A_t = torch.as_tensor(A)
        mu_q, _ = model.encode(S_t, A_t)
        A_hat = model.decode(mu_q, S_t)
        return float(((A_t - A_hat) ** 2).sum(-1).mean())


def eval_policy_replacement(model, S, A_actual):
    with torch.no_grad():
        S_t = torch.as_tensor(S)
        A_vae = model.act_deterministic(S_t).numpy()
        return float(((A_actual - A_vae) ** 2).sum(-1).mean()), A_vae


def eval_rollout(model, seeds, max_steps=1000, stochastic=False):
    from mw_to_protomotions.cem_policy_search import get_features
    results = []
    for seed in seeds:
        env = gym.make("Walker2d-v5", exclude_current_positions_from_observation=False)
        obs, info = env.reset(seed=seed)
        data = env.unwrapped.data
        steps = 0
        for _ in range(max_steps):
            s = torch.as_tensor(get_features(data)[None], dtype=torch.float32)
            act = model.act_stochastic(s) if stochastic else model.act_deterministic(s)
            act = act.numpy()[0]
            obs, r, term, trunc, info = env.step(act)
            steps += 1
            if term:
                break
        env.close()
        results.append(steps)
    return np.array(results)


# ── main ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("EXP_03 / E1 — VAE as Policy, Compression Test")
    print("=" * 68)

    # Load ground-truth policy
    theta_path = "cem_best_theta.npy"
    if not os.path.exists(theta_path):
        sys.exit(f"ERROR: {theta_path} not found — run ./run_plan_a.sh first")
    theta = np.load(theta_path)
    print(f"loaded {theta_path}, |theta| = {np.linalg.norm(theta):.3f}\n")

    # Generate training + held-out data
    print("[1/5] collecting training data (100 rollouts)…")
    t0 = time.time()
    S_train, A_train = collect(theta, seeds=list(range(0, 100)))
    print(f"  {len(S_train)} (s, a) pairs, {time.time()-t0:.1f}s\n")

    print("[2/5] collecting held-out data (20 rollouts)…")
    S_held, A_held = collect(theta, seeds=list(range(1000, 1020)))
    print(f"  {len(S_held)} (s, a) pairs\n")

    # Train CVAE
    print(f"[3/5] training CVAE (latent_dim=8, hidden=64, beta=0.01)…")
    t0 = time.time()
    model = train_cvae(S_train, A_train, latent_dim=8, hidden=64, beta=0.01)
    print(f"  trained in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s\n")

    # Evaluate
    print("[4/5] evaluating compression…")
    recon_err = eval_reconstruction(model, S_held, A_held)
    print(f"  reconstruction error (held-out):  {recon_err:.4f}")
    print(f"    (target: < 0.05 for 'VAE sees the data')")

    repl_err, A_vae = eval_policy_replacement(model, S_held, A_held)
    print(f"  policy replacement error:         {repl_err:.4f}")
    print(f"    (target: < 0.10 for 'VAE IS the policy')")

    print("\n[5/5] evaluating rollouts…")
    det_steps = eval_rollout(model, list(range(2000, 2020)), stochastic=False)
    sto_steps = eval_rollout(model, list(range(2000, 2020)), stochastic=True)
    print(f"  deterministic (z = prior_mu):  mean {det_steps.mean():.1f} / 1000")
    print(f"  stochastic    (z ~ prior):     mean {sto_steps.mean():.1f} / 1000")
    print(f"  original policy reference:     920.9 / 1000")

    # Save
    torch.save({
        "state_dict": model.state_dict(),
        "config": dict(state_dim=15, action_dim=6, latent_dim=8, hidden=64),
    }, "exp_03_cvae_balance.pt")
    print(f"\nsaved model to exp_03_cvae_balance.pt")

    # Verdict
    print("\n" + "=" * 68)
    print("VERDICT")
    print("=" * 68)
    if recon_err < 0.05 and repl_err < 0.10 and det_steps.mean() >= 900:
        print("STRONG — VAE is a valid policy object.")
        print("  → proceed to E2 (latent search)")
    elif recon_err < 0.05 and repl_err < 0.30 and det_steps.mean() >= 500:
        print("MODERATE — VAE represents the policy, but lossily.")
        print("  → sweep latent_dim (4, 8, 16) and beta (0.01, 0.1, 0.5)")
    elif recon_err < 0.05 and repl_err >= 0.30:
        print("AMBIGUOUS — VAE encodes (s,a) but cannot act from s alone.")
        print("  → diagnose: which failure mode from EXP_03 §6?")
        print("    Likely β too low. Re-run with β = 0.1, 0.5, 1.0.")
    elif recon_err >= 0.10:
        print("WEAK — VAE capacity is insufficient.")
        print("  → increase hidden (128) and latent_dim (16)")
    else:
        print("FAIL — VAE cannot represent this policy.")
        print("  → the policy-as-manifold idea requires a different architecture")
    print("=" * 68)

===== domain/exp_e2b_vae_walk_policy.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""EXP_03 / E2b — §4 follow-up: train the same CVAE recipe on the WALK policy
(cem_walk_theta.npy) and check whether the CVAE walks. Original walk policy is
evaluated on the same seeds for a fair baseline."""
import sys, os, time
sys.path.insert(0, os.getcwd()); sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import numpy as np, gymnasium as gym, torch
from exp_e1_vae_policy_compress import collect, train_cvae, eval_policy_replacement
from exp_e2_vae_policy_transfer import eval_walk
from mw_to_protomotions.cem_policy_search import get_features, policy_action

def walk_original(theta, seeds, max_steps=600):
    fwd, steps = [], []
    for seed in seeds:
        env = gym.make("Walker2d-v5", exclude_current_positions_from_observation=False)
        env.reset(seed=seed); d = env.unwrapped.data; x0 = d.qpos[0]; n = 0
        for _ in range(max_steps):
            _, _, term, _, _ = env.step(policy_action(theta, get_features(d))); n += 1
            if term: break
        fwd.append(float(d.qpos[0] - x0)); steps.append(n); env.close()
    return np.array(fwd), np.array(steps)

theta = np.load("cem_walk_theta.npy")
t0 = time.time()
S, A = collect(theta, seeds=list(range(0, 100)), max_steps=600)
Sh, Ah = collect(theta, seeds=list(range(1000, 1020)), max_steps=600)
print(f"collected {len(S)} train / {len(Sh)} held-out pairs in {time.time()-t0:.0f}s", flush=True)
model = train_cvae(S, A, latent_dim=8, hidden=64, beta=0.01)
err, _ = eval_policy_replacement(model, Sh, Ah)
print(f"policy replacement error (held-out): {err:.4f}")
seeds = list(range(3000, 3020))
fo, so = walk_original(theta, seeds)
fc, sc = eval_walk(model, seeds)
print(f"original walk policy : fwd {fo.mean():.2f} m (median {np.median(fo):.2f}), steps {so.mean():.1f}/600")
print(f"walk-trained CVAE    : fwd {fc.mean():.2f} m (median {np.median(fc):.2f}), steps {sc.mean():.1f}/600")
print(f"ratio fwd CVAE/orig  : {fc.mean()/fo.mean():.2f}")
torch.save({"state_dict": model.state_dict(),
            "config": dict(state_dim=15, action_dim=6, latent_dim=8, hidden=64)}, "exp_03_cvae_walk.pt")

===== domain/exp_e2_rank4_humanoid_vel.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
E2 - Rank-4 humanoid with root linear velocities added to features.
Everything else identical to rank4_humanoid.py.
"""
import sys, os, time
import numpy as np
import gymnasium as gym

ACT_DIM = 17
FEAT_DIM = 45  # was 42; +3 for qvel[0:3] (vx, vy, vz)
RANK = 4
DIM = RANK * FEAT_DIM + ACT_DIM * RANK + ACT_DIM  # 265


def get_features(data):
    return np.concatenate([
        [data.qpos[2]],
        data.qpos[3:7],
        data.qvel[0:3],    # NEW: root linear velocities
        data.qpos[7:24],
        data.qvel[3:6],
        data.qvel[6:23],
    ])


def unpack(theta):
    i = RANK * FEAT_DIM
    j = i + ACT_DIM * RANK
    V = theta[:i].reshape(RANK, FEAT_DIM)
    U = theta[i:j].reshape(ACT_DIM, RANK)
    b = theta[j:]
    return V, U, b


def policy_action(theta, feat):
    V, U, b = unpack(theta)
    z = V @ feat
    return 0.4 * np.tanh(U @ z + b)


def rollout(theta, env, seed, max_steps):
    obs, info = env.reset(seed=seed)
    data = env.unwrapped.data
    steps = 0
    for _ in range(max_steps):
        feat = get_features(data)
        act = policy_action(theta, feat)
        obs, r, term, trunc, info = env.step(act)
        steps += 1
        if term:
            break
    return steps


def fitness(theta, env, seeds, max_steps):
    return np.mean([rollout(theta, env, s, max_steps) for s in seeds])


def cem(pop_size=80, generations=30, max_steps=400, n_eval_seeds=3, seed=0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    mean = np.zeros(DIM); std = np.ones(DIM) * 0.3
    n_elite = max(1, int(pop_size * 0.2))
    for gen in range(generations):
        pop = mean + std * rng.standard_normal((pop_size, DIM))
        gen_seeds = rng.integers(0, 100_000, size=n_eval_seeds).tolist()
        fits = np.array([fitness(t, env, gen_seeds, max_steps) for t in pop])
        elite = pop[np.argsort(fits)[-n_elite:]]
        mean, std = elite.mean(0), elite.std(0) + 1e-3
        print(f"  gen {gen:2d}  pop_mean={fits.mean():7.1f}  "
              f"pop_best={fits.max():7.1f}  std_norm={np.linalg.norm(std):.3f}",
              flush=True)
    env.close()
    return mean


if __name__ == "__main__":
    gens = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 30
    print(f"E2 - rank-4 + velocity features on Humanoid-v5")
    print(f"  DIM={DIM} (was 253 without velocities)")
    print(f"  budget: pop=80, gens={gens}, max_steps=400")
    print(f"  baseline: rank4_humanoid.py @ 30 gen = 99.0, @ 90 gen = 112.7")
    print()

    t0 = time.time()
    best_theta = cem(generations=gens)
    print(f"\nCEM finished in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s")

    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    held_out = fitness(best_theta, env, list(range(100, 120)), max_steps=1000)
    env.close()
    print(f"\nHeld-out mean (20 seeds, cap 1000): {held_out:.1f} steps")
    print(f"  rank4 baseline (30 gens, no vel): 99.0")
    print(f"  rank4 baseline (90 gens, no vel): 112.7")
    print(f"  random baseline: 26.1")

    np.save("exp_e2_rank4_humanoid_vel_theta.npy", best_theta)

===== domain/exp_e2_vae_policy_transfer.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
EXP_03 / E2 — Does the balance-trained CVAE generalise to a walk task?
Tests whether "VAE as policy" generalises when the task changes.

Loads exp_03_cvae_balance.pt, runs it as a policy on Walker2d with the
forward-walk task. Expected outcomes:
  - ≥ 1.0 m forward: the CVAE learned general Walker2d behavior
  - 0.2–1.0 m: partial transfer, the manifold has a "good action" prior
  - < 0.2 m but survives 500+: it stands, doesn't walk (expected, not a failure)
  - < 0.2 m and falls: overfit to balance, diagnose
"""
import sys, os
sys.path.insert(0, os.getcwd())
import numpy as np
import gymnasium as gym
import torch

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
from exp_e1_vae_policy_compress import CVAE, eval_rollout
from mw_to_protomotions.cem_policy_search import get_features


def eval_walk(model, seeds, max_steps=600):
    """Runs the CVAE as a policy with forward-distance measurement."""
    fwd, steps = [], []
    for seed in seeds:
        env = gym.make("Walker2d-v5", exclude_current_positions_from_observation=False)
        obs, info = env.reset(seed=seed)
        data = env.unwrapped.data
        x0 = data.qpos[0]
        s_count = 0
        for _ in range(max_steps):
            s = torch.as_tensor(get_features(data)[None], dtype=torch.float32)
            act = model.act_deterministic(s).numpy()[0]
            obs, r, term, trunc, info = env.step(act)
            s_count += 1
            if term:
                break
        fwd.append(float(data.qpos[0] - x0))
        steps.append(s_count)
        env.close()
    return np.array(fwd), np.array(steps)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("EXP_03 / E2 — VAE Policy Transfer: balance-trained CVAE on walk task")
    print("=" * 68)

    # Load the CVAE from E1
    path = "exp_03_cvae_balance.pt"
    if not os.path.exists(path):
        sys.exit(f"ERROR: {path} not found — run exp_e1_vae_policy_compress.py first")
    ck = torch.load(path, map_location="cpu", weights_only=False)
    model = CVAE(**ck["config"])
    model.load_state_dict(ck["state_dict"])
    model.eval()
    print(f"loaded CVAE from {path}\n")

    # Run the CVAE as a policy on the walk task
    print("[1/2] running balance-trained CVAE on walk task (20 fresh seeds)…")
    fwd, steps = eval_walk(model, seeds=list(range(3000, 3020)))
    print(f"  mean forward: {fwd.mean():.2f} m")
    print(f"  mean steps:   {steps.mean():.1f} / 600")
    print(f"  reference:    linear walk policy = 1.33 m, "
          f"linear balance policy ≈ -0.75 m\n")

    # Verdict
    print("[2/2] verdict")
    print("=" * 68)
    if fwd.mean() >= 1.0:
        print("TRANSFERS — CVAE walks without walk-specific training.")
        print("  → the balance-trained manifold contains general walker2d behavior")
        print("  → strong evidence for 'VAE as policy, trained on any good data'")
    elif fwd.mean() >= 0.2:
        print("PARTIAL — CVAE moves forward somewhat without being trained to.")
        print("  → likely the CVAE encodes 'standing upright' behavior which "
              "translates to slow forward lean")
        print("  → train a walk-trained CVAE next for a real comparison")
    elif steps.mean() >= 500:
        print("STANDS — CVAE holds balance but doesn't walk.")
        print("  → this is expected; the balance CVAE was never trained to walk")
        print("  → the interesting follow-up: train CVAE on walk data, "
              "does it walk?")
    else:
        print("FAILS — CVAE cannot act as a walk policy.")
        print("  → check whether act_deterministic(s) produces "
              "physically-reasonable actions on walk-task states")
        print("  → likely the balance-trained distribution is too narrow")
    print("=" * 68)

===== domain/exp_e3_rank4_humanoid_reward.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
E3 - Rank-4 humanoid with env-reward-sum fitness instead of survival steps.
Everything else identical to rank4_humanoid.py.
"""
import sys, os, time
import numpy as np
import gymnasium as gym

ACT_DIM = 17
FEAT_DIM = 42   # original features, NO velocities - E2 tested that separately
RANK = 4
DIM = RANK * FEAT_DIM + ACT_DIM * RANK + ACT_DIM   # 253


def get_features(data):
    return np.concatenate([
        [data.qpos[2]],
        data.qpos[3:7],
        data.qpos[7:24],
        data.qvel[3:6],
        data.qvel[6:23],
    ])


def unpack(theta):
    i = RANK * FEAT_DIM
    j = i + ACT_DIM * RANK
    V = theta[:i].reshape(RANK, FEAT_DIM)
    U = theta[i:j].reshape(ACT_DIM, RANK)
    b = theta[j:]
    return V, U, b


def policy_action(theta, feat):
    V, U, b = unpack(theta)
    z = V @ feat
    return 0.4 * np.tanh(U @ z + b)


def rollout_reward(theta, env, seed, max_steps):
    obs, info = env.reset(seed=seed)
    data = env.unwrapped.data
    total_r = 0.0
    steps = 0
    for _ in range(max_steps):
        feat = get_features(data)
        act = policy_action(theta, feat)
        obs, r, term, trunc, info = env.step(act)
        total_r += float(r)
        steps += 1
        if term:
            break
    return total_r, steps


def fitness_reward(theta, env, seeds, max_steps):
    return float(np.mean([rollout_reward(theta, env, s, max_steps)[0] for s in seeds]))


def cem(pop_size=80, generations=30, max_steps=400, n_eval_seeds=3, seed=0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    mean = np.zeros(DIM); std = np.ones(DIM) * 0.3
    n_elite = max(1, int(pop_size * 0.2))
    for gen in range(generations):
        pop = mean + std * rng.standard_normal((pop_size, DIM))
        gen_seeds = rng.integers(0, 100_000, size=n_eval_seeds).tolist()
        fits = np.array([fitness_reward(t, env, gen_seeds, max_steps) for t in pop])
        elite = pop[np.argsort(fits)[-n_elite:]]
        mean, std = elite.mean(0), elite.std(0) + 1e-3
        print(f"  gen {gen:2d}  mean_reward={fits.mean():9.1f}  "
              f"best_reward={fits.max():9.1f}  std_norm={np.linalg.norm(std):.3f}",
              flush=True)
    env.close()
    return mean


if __name__ == "__main__":
    gens = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 30
    print(f"E3 - rank-4 + env-reward-sum fitness on Humanoid-v5")
    print(f"  DIM={DIM}, budget: pop=80, gens={gens}, max_steps=400")
    print(f"  baseline: rank4_humanoid.py @ 30 gen = 99.0")
    print()

    t0 = time.time()
    best_theta = cem(generations=gens)
    print(f"\nCEM finished in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s")

    env = gym.make("Humanoid-v5")
    held_out_seeds = list(range(100, 120))
    rewards, steps = [], []
    for s in held_out_seeds:
        r, st = rollout_reward(best_theta, env, s, max_steps=1000)
        rewards.append(r); steps.append(st)
    env.close()
    print(f"\nHeld-out (20 seeds, cap 1000):")
    print(f"  mean sum_reward = {np.mean(rewards):9.1f}")
    print(f"  mean steps      = {np.mean(steps):6.1f}   (baseline 99.0)")
    print(f"  random baseline steps = 26.1")

    np.save("exp_e3_rank4_humanoid_reward_theta.npy", best_theta)

===== domain/inverse.py =====
"""
mw_framework.query.inverse
============================
inverse_query(det, s, s') → a* — solve for the action that most plausibly
carried state s to state s' under the learned transition manifold.

Method: small CEM over the action block only, minimizing MW distance to the
manifold. m = 6 for Walker2d, ~30 for Humanoid. n_pop = 32, n_gen = 3 →
96 VAE forward passes. ~30ms on a commodity CPU core.

This is the primitive Plan B substitutes for a policy network: instead of
"policy(s) → a", we ask "which a makes (s, a, s') look like a real transition?"
"""
from __future__ import annotations
import numpy as np
import torch
from typing import Optional


def _build_query_batch(det, s_row: dict, a_batch: torch.Tensor,
                        sp_row: dict) -> torch.Tensor:
    """Assemble (N, n_features) batch: s fixed, a varies, s' fixed.
    Returns a NORMALISED tensor on the model's device."""
    n = a_batch.shape[0]
    from ..domains.transition import STATE_FEATURES, ACTION_FEATURES
    # physical rows
    s_vec = np.array([s_row[f] for f in STATE_FEATURES], dtype=np.float32)
    sp_vec = np.array([sp_row[f] for f in STATE_FEATURES], dtype=np.float32)
    a_np = a_batch.detach().cpu().numpy().astype(np.float32)
    s_block = np.tile(s_vec, (n, 1))
    sp_block = np.tile(sp_vec, (n, 1))
    full = np.concatenate([s_block, a_np, sp_block], axis=1)
    xn = det.norm_.transform(full)
    dev = next(det.vae_.parameters()).device
    return torch.as_tensor(xn, dtype=torch.float32, device=dev)


def inverse_query(det, s_row: dict, sp_row: dict,
                  n_pop: int = 32, n_gen: int = 3, elite_frac: float = 0.2,
                  init_std: float = 0.5, seed: Optional[int] = None) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return a* (physical units) that minimises MW distance to the manifold
    for the triple (s_row, a*, sp_row).

    s_row / sp_row: dicts keyed by STATE_FEATURES (e.g. {"height": 1.25, ...}).
    Returns: numpy array of 6 torques, physical units, clipped to ±A_MAX.
    """
    from ..domains.transition import ACTION_FEATURES, A_MAX
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    m = len(ACTION_FEATURES)
    n_elite = max(1, int(n_pop * elite_frac))

    mean = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float32)
    std = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float32) * init_std

    for _ in range(n_gen):
        # sample in physical action units directly (not normalised)
        pop = mean + std * rng.standard_normal((n_pop, m))
        pop = np.clip(pop, -A_MAX, A_MAX)
        a_t = torch.as_tensor(pop, dtype=torch.float32)
        xbatch = _build_query_batch(det, s_row, a_t, sp_row)
        with torch.no_grad():
            dist, _ = det.vae_.mw_distance(xbatch, mc_samples=2)
        d = dist.cpu().numpy()
        elite_idx = np.argsort(d)[:n_elite]
        elite = pop[elite_idx]
        mean = elite.mean(axis=0)
        std = elite.std(axis=0) + 1e-3

    return np.clip(mean, -A_MAX, A_MAX).astype(np.float32)

===== domain/test_transition.py =====
"""
tests/test_transition_gate
============================
The single decisive test for Plan B. If this passes, Plan B is viable and
worth building the full stack. If it fails, Plan B is structurally limited
(the optimal action is not on the manifold) and Plan A — CEM over policy
parameters — remains the honest path.

The test:
  1. Train TransitionVAE on ~50k real Walker2d transitions.
  2. On held-out (s, s') pairs, solve for a* via inverse_query.
  3. Compare a* to the actual action a that produced each pair.
  4. If ||a* - a|| is small relative to the action range: the inverse is
     recoverable → the optimal action is on the manifold → Plan B is viable.

Threshold justification: a random action has expected error
~0.29 in units of A_MAX = 1.0 (uniform [-1,1] RMS vs mean-zero). A recoverable
inverse should be at least 2× better than random → mean error < 0.15.
A *tight* recoverable inverse should be ≤ 0.1 × A_MAX.
"""
import os, sys
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))

import numpy as np
import pytest
import pyro

from mw_framework import MWDetector, set_seed
from mw_framework.domains.transition import (
    TransitionVAE, STATE_FEATURES, ACTION_FEATURES, FEATURES)
from mw_framework.query.inverse import inverse_query


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def trained_detector():
    """Train once for the module: ~50k Walker2d transitions, 200 epochs.
    Cached on disk so re-runs don't retrain (~3-5 min on a laptop CPU)."""
    from mw_to_protomotions.transition_datagen import collect_transitions
    cache = "/tmp/mw_transition_walker.ckpt"
    if os.path.exists(cache):
        return MWDetector.load(cache)
    set_seed(0); pyro.clear_param_store()
    df = collect_transitions(n_units=20, n_steps=250, seed0=100)
    # 80/20 split
    n = len(df)
    train = df.iloc[: int(n * 0.8)]
    det = MWDetector(vae=TransitionVAE(), epochs=200, lr=2e-3).fit(train[FEATURES])
    det.save(cache)
    return det


def test_manifold_learns_cleanly(trained_detector):
    """Sanity: the transition manifold has a low reconstruction floor on
    held-out normal transitions. If this fails, nothing else is meaningful."""
    from mw_to_protomotions.transition_datagen import collect_transitions
    held = collect_transitions(n_units=5, n_steps=250, seed0=9000)
    d = trained_detector.mw_distance(held[FEATURES])
    # Baseline calibrated on training data. Held-out should be within 2×.
    assert d.mean() < 2.0 * trained_detector.calibration_.mu + 1.0


def test_e_gate_inverse_recoverable(trained_detector):
    """The decisive test. Solve for a* given (s, s') on held-out triples.
    If mean ||a* - a_actual|| is well below random, the optimal action is
    on the manifold."""
    from mw_to_protomotions.transition_datagen import collect_transitions
    held = collect_transitions(n_units=5, n_steps=250, seed0=9000)
    held = held.sample(n=min(200, len(held)), random_state=0).reset_index(drop=True)

    errors = []
    for _, row in held.iterrows():
        s_row = {f: float(row[f"s_{f}"]) for f in STATE_FEATURES}
        sp_row = {f: float(row[f"sp_{f}"]) for f in STATE_FEATURES}
        a_actual = np.array([float(row[f]) for f in ACTION_FEATURES], dtype=np.float32)
        a_star = inverse_query(trained_detector, s_row, sp_row,
                                n_pop=32, n_gen=3, seed=int(row["step"]))
        errors.append(float(np.linalg.norm(a_star - a_actual)))
    errors = np.array(errors)

    mean_err = float(errors.mean())
    std_err = float(errors.std())
    p90 = float(np.percentile(errors, 90))

    # Random-action baseline for comparison (uniform in [-1,1]^6)
    random_errs = []
    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
    for _ in range(200):
        a1 = rng.uniform(-1, 1, 6)
        a2 = rng.uniform(-1, 1, 6)
        random_errs.append(float(np.linalg.norm(a1 - a2)))
    random_mean = float(np.mean(random_errs))

    print(f"\n  E-gate: inverse_query action error")
    print(f"    mean  = {mean_err:.4f}")
    print(f"    std   = {std_err:.4f}")
    print(f"    p90   = {p90:.4f}")
    print(f"    random baseline mean = {random_mean:.4f}")
    print(f"    improvement over random = {random_mean / max(mean_err, 1e-6):.2f}x")

    # The honest thresholds.
    # 1. Mean error must be at least 2× better than random.
    assert mean_err < 0.5 * random_mean, (
        f"E-gate FAILED: mean error {mean_err:.3f} is not 2× better than "
        f"random {random_mean:.3f}. Plan B is not viable on this robot as spec'd."
    )
    # 2. Std must be bounded (not catastrophic variance)
    assert std_err < 0.5 * random_mean, (
        f"E-gate FAILED: std {std_err:.3f} too large. The inverse is ill-posed."
    )
    # 3. Report the "strong success" signal as a warning, not a hard fail
    if mean_err < 0.1:
        print(f"  STRONG: mean error {mean_err:.4f} < 0.1 — Plan B is a clear win.")
    elif mean_err < 0.2:
        print(f"  MODERATE: mean error {mean_err:.4f} in [0.1, 0.2] — Plan B "
              f"is viable but Plan A may match it. Run V10 before committing.")


def test_e_gate_does_not_degrade_under_noise(trained_detector):
    """Plan B must be robust to sensor noise at inference. Add σ=0.05
    Gaussian noise to s and s' in the query; the inverse should still
    recover a*."""
    from mw_to_protomotions.transition_datagen import collect_transitions
    held = collect_transitions(n_units=3, n_steps=250, seed0=9000)
    held = held.sample(n=50, random_state=1).reset_index(drop=True)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(42)

    errors = []
    for _, row in held.iterrows():
        s_row = {f: float(row[f"s_{f}"]) + rng.normal(0, 0.05) for f in STATE_FEATURES}
        sp_row = {f: float(row[f"sp_{f}"]) + rng.normal(0, 0.05) for f in STATE_FEATURES}
        a_actual = np.array([float(row[f]) for f in ACTION_FEATURES], dtype=np.float32)
        a_star = inverse_query(trained_detector, s_row, sp_row,
                                n_pop=32, n_gen=3, seed=0)
        errors.append(float(np.linalg.norm(a_star - a_actual)))
    mean_err = float(np.mean(errors))
    print(f"\n  E-gate with σ=0.05 noise: mean error = {mean_err:.4f}")
    # Loose threshold: noise degrades the inverse but it should still be
    # materially better than random.
    assert mean_err < 0.7, f"Noise robustness failed: {mean_err:.3f} >= 0.7"

===== domain/transition_datagen.py =====
"""
mw_to_protomotions/transition_datagen
======================================
Emit (s, a, s') transitions from real MuJoCo rollouts, using the
pybamm_like recipe from mujoco_experiments/03 — many independent units,
continuously varied physics parameters.
"""
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import gymnasium as gym
import mujoco
from mw_framework.domains.transition import (
    STATE_FEATURES, ACTION_FEATURES, FEATURES)


def _state_row(data) -> dict:
    return {
        "height": float(data.qpos[1]),
        "pitch": float(data.qpos[2]),
        "pitch_rate": float(data.qvel[2]),
        "thigh": float(data.qpos[3]), "leg": float(data.qpos[4]),
        "foot": float(data.qpos[5]),
        "thigh_left": float(data.qpos[6]), "leg_left": float(data.qpos[7]),
        "foot_left": float(data.qpos[8]),
    }


def collect_transitions(n_units: int = 20, n_steps: int = 250,
                        seed0: int = 100,
                        friction_range=(0.4, 1.3),
                        damping_range=(0.05, 0.35)) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """One 'unit' = one MuJoCo instance with its own sampled physics.
    Runs the PD controller from datagen.py, logging every transition."""
    kp = np.array([4.0, 4.0, 2.0, 4.0, 4.0, 2.0])
    kd = np.array([0.5, 0.5, 0.2, 0.5, 0.5, 0.2])
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed0)
    rows = []
    for unit in range(n_units):
        fr = rng.uniform(*friction_range)
        dp = rng.uniform(*damping_range)
        env = gym.make("Walker2d-v5", exclude_current_positions_from_observation=False)
        env.reset(seed=seed0 + unit)
        m, d = env.unwrapped.model, env.unwrapped.data
        m.geom_friction[0, 0] = fr
        m.dof_damping[3:9] = dp
        for step in range(n_steps):
            s = _state_row(d)
            pitch, pitch_rate = s["pitch"], s["pitch_rate"]
            bal = -1.0 * (6.0 * pitch + 1.0 * pitch_rate)
            ref = np.zeros(6); ref[2] = bal; ref[5] = bal
            a = np.clip(kp * (ref - d.qpos[3:9]) - kd * d.qvel[3:9], -1, 1)
            _, _, term, trunc, _ = env.step(a)
            sp = _state_row(d)
            row = {f"s_{k}": s[k] for k in STATE_FEATURES}
            row.update({ACTION_FEATURES[i]: float(a[i]) for i in range(6)})
            row.update({f"sp_{k}": sp[k] for k in STATE_FEATURES})
            row["unit"] = unit
            row["step"] = step
            row["friction"] = fr
            row["damping"] = dp
            rows.append(row)
            if term or trunc:
                break
        env.close()
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)

===== domain/transition.py =====
"""
mw_framework.domains.transition
=================================
A transition manifold over (state, action, next_state) — Plan B's core object.

Unlike walker_domain.py's WalkerPostureVAE (which learns a manifold over STATES
only), this learns the manifold over TRANSITIONS. The physics priors make the
decoder's output lie on the admissible-transition manifold. The joint query
"given s and s', what a?" then becomes a MW-distance minimization problem,
solved by a small CEM over the action block only (6 dims for Walker2d, ~30
for Humanoid) — cheap enough for real-time CPU.

This is the object Plan B replaces a policy network with. There is no learned
controller; the manifold IS the model, and control is a posterior sample.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
from typing import Dict
import torch
from ..core import MWVAE, constraint, bound

# 24 features: 9 state + 6 action + 9 next_state
STATE_FEATURES = ["height", "pitch", "pitch_rate",
                  "thigh", "leg", "foot",
                  "thigh_left", "leg_left", "foot_left"]
ACTION_FEATURES = ["a_thigh", "a_leg", "a_foot",
                   "a_thigh_left", "a_leg_left", "a_foot_left"]
FEATURES = (["s_" + f for f in STATE_FEATURES]
            + ACTION_FEATURES
            + ["sp_" + f for f in STATE_FEATURES])

# Walker2d-v5 physical bounds (from MuJoCo jnt_range / actuator_forcerange)
THIGH_LIM = (-2.61799388, 0.0)
LEG_LIM = (-2.61799388, 0.0)
FOOT_LIM = (-0.78539816, 0.78539816)
A_MAX = 1.0

# One-step reachability priors (loose but anti-teleport)
# Walker2d-v5 dt=0.008; a joint can move at most ~20 rad/s comfortably.
DT = 0.008
JOINT_DELTA_MAX = 20.0 * DT   # 0.16 rad
HEIGHT_DELTA_MAX = 5.0 * DT   # 0.04 m
PITCH_DELTA_MAX = 10.0 * DT   # 0.08 rad


class TransitionVAE(MWVAE):
    """Joint VAE over (s, a, s'). Physics priors: joint limits, torque limits,
    one-step reachability, height/pitch envelope."""

    def __init__(self, latent_dim: int = 8, hidden_dim: int = 128,
                 dropout: float = 0.05, law_tolerance: float = 0.05, **kwargs):
        kwargs.setdefault("n_features", len(FEATURES))
        kwargs.setdefault("feature_names", FEATURES)
        kwargs.setdefault("obs_sigma", [0.05] * len(FEATURES))
        super().__init__(latent_dim=latent_dim, hidden_dim=hidden_dim,
                         dropout=dropout, **kwargs)
        self.law_tolerance = law_tolerance

    def get_config(self):
        cfg = super().get_config()
        cfg.update(law_tolerance=self.law_tolerance)
        return cfg

    def physics(self, x: Dict[str, torch.Tensor]) -> None:
        tol = self.law_tolerance
        # C1 — exact joint limits on s and s'
        for prefix in ("s_", "sp_"):
            for j, (lo, hi) in [("thigh", THIGH_LIM), ("leg", LEG_LIM),
                                ("foot", FOOT_LIM), ("thigh_left", THIGH_LIM),
                                ("leg_left", LEG_LIM), ("foot_left", FOOT_LIM)]:
                bound(f"c1_{prefix}{j}", x[f"{prefix}{j}"], low=lo, high=hi, tolerance=tol)

        # C2 — actuator torque limits
        for name in ACTION_FEATURES:
            bound(f"c2_{name}", x[name], low=-A_MAX, high=A_MAX, tolerance=tol)

        # C3 — one-step kinematic reachability: |s' - s| <= bound (anti-teleport)
        for j in STATE_FEATURES:
            if j in ("height", "pitch", "pitch_rate"):
                dmax = HEIGHT_DELTA_MAX if j == "height" else PITCH_DELTA_MAX
            else:
                dmax = JOINT_DELTA_MAX
            delta = (x[f"sp_{j}"] - x[f"s_{j}"]).abs()
            bound(f"c3_reach_{j}", delta, high=dmax, tolerance=2 * tol)

        # C4 — pitch envelope (soft; the controller's own stable range)
        bound("c4_s_pitch", x["s_pitch"].abs(), high=1.5, tolerance=tol)
        bound("c4_sp_pitch", x["sp_pitch"].abs(), high=1.5, tolerance=tol)
        # C4 — height cannot be negative (no body under the floor)
        bound("c4_s_height_low", x["s_height"], low=0.0, tolerance=tol)
        bound("c4_sp_height_low", x["sp_height"], low=0.0, tolerance=tol)

    @staticmethod
    def residuals(x: Dict[str, torch.Tensor]) -> Dict[str, torch.Tensor]:
        """Useful summary residuals for diagnostics / failure analysis."""
        return {
            "reach_hip": (x["sp_thigh"] - x["s_thigh"]).abs(),
            "reach_knee": (x["sp_leg"] - x["s_leg"]).abs(),
            "height_delta": (x["sp_height"] - x["s_height"]).abs(),
            "pitch_delta": (x["sp_pitch"] - x["s_pitch"]).abs(),
        }


# domain/walker_demo/                                                   

===== domain/walker_demo/walker_animation.py =====
"""Analogous to robot_motion.py — but for Walker2d, the frames are real
MuJoCo qpos/qvel (real physics), and MW distance is computed per step via
the trained WalkerPostureVAE. The manifold is the gauge, not the animation."""
from __future__ import annotations
import os, sys
_HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
_DOMAIN = os.path.dirname(_HERE)
_REPO = os.path.dirname(_DOMAIN)
sys.path.insert(0, _DOMAIN)
sys.path.insert(0, _REPO)
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(_REPO, "mw_to_protomotions"))  # same fix as walker_app.py

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import gymnasium as gym


def run_policy_rollout(theta, max_steps=1000, push_at=-1, push=0.0,
                       det=None, seed=0):
    """Real MuJoCo rollout driven by a CEM policy. Returns state stream +
    MW distance stream + summary. Schema matches the Flask demo's
    {frames, domain, equations} but with qpos/qvel as the frame content."""
    from mw_to_protomotions.cem_policy_search import get_features, policy_action

    env = gym.make("Walker2d-v5",
                   exclude_current_positions_from_observation=False)
    obs, info = env.reset(seed=seed)
    data = env.unwrapped.data
    x0 = data.qpos[0]

    qpos_log, qvel_log, posture_rows = [], [], []
    steps = 0
    fell_at = None

    for t in range(max_steps):
        act = policy_action(theta, get_features(data))
        if t == push_at:
            data.qvel[0] += push

        qpos_log.append([float(v) for v in data.qpos])
        qvel_log.append([float(v) for v in data.qvel])
        posture_rows.append({
            "height": float(data.qpos[1]),
            "pitch":  float(data.qpos[2]),
            "pitch_rate": float(data.qvel[2]),
            "thigh":      float(data.qpos[3]), "leg":      float(data.qpos[4]),
            "foot":       float(data.qpos[5]),
            "thigh_left": float(data.qpos[6]), "leg_left": float(data.qpos[7]),
            "foot_left":  float(data.qpos[8]),
        })

        obs, r, term, trunc, info = env.step(act)
        steps += 1
        if term:
            fell_at = t
            break
    env.close()

    mw_distances = []
    mw_error = None
    if det is None:
        mw_error = "detector_not_loaded"
    elif posture_rows:
        from walker_domain import FEATURES
        try:
            df_posture = pd.DataFrame(posture_rows)[FEATURES]
            d = det.mw_distance(df_posture)
            mw_distances = [float(v) for v in d]
        except Exception as e:
            import traceback
            mw_error = f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}"
            print(f"[MW] mw_distance failed: {mw_error}", flush=True)
            traceback.print_exc()

    return {
        "qpos": qpos_log,
        "qvel": qvel_log,
        "mw_distances": mw_distances,
        "mw_error": mw_error,
        "steps": steps,
        "forward_distance": float(data.qpos[0] - x0),
        "fell_at": fell_at,
        "seed": seed,
        "push_at": push_at,
        "push": push,
    }

===== domain/walker_demo/walker_app.py =====
"""Analogous to full_flow_animation/app.py. Same schema, same route shape,
two policies (balance / walk) plus a push test."""
from __future__ import annotations
import os, sys
_HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
_DOMAIN = os.path.dirname(_HERE)
_REPO = os.path.dirname(_DOMAIN)
sys.path.insert(0, _HERE)
sys.path.insert(0, _DOMAIN)
sys.path.insert(0, _REPO)
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(_REPO, "mw_to_protomotions"))  # walker_domain.py lives
# here, and the checkpoint was saved expecting it importable as a top-level
# module (not mw_to_protomotions.walker_domain) - the real bug behind the
# "silent" MW failure: MWDetector.load() raised ModuleNotFoundError for
# 'walker_domain' because this path was missing, not a schema mismatch.

import numpy as np
from flask import Flask, jsonify, request, send_from_directory
from walker_animation import run_policy_rollout
from mw_framework import MWDetector

app = Flask(__name__,
            static_folder=os.path.join(_HERE, "static"),
            static_url_path="")

POLICIES = {}
for name, relpath in [("balance", "cem_best_theta.npy"),
                      ("walk", "cem_walk_theta.npy")]:
    path = os.path.join(_REPO, relpath)
    if os.path.exists(path):
        POLICIES[name] = np.load(path)
        print(f"loaded policy '{name}' from {relpath}")

DET = None
DET_PATH = None
det_candidates = [
    os.path.join(_REPO, "mw_to_protomotions", "walker_mw_detector.ckpt"),
    os.path.join(_REPO, "walker_mw_detector.ckpt"),
]
for p in det_candidates:
    if not os.path.exists(p):
        continue
    try:
        candidate = MWDetector.load(p)
        # ── validate on a known-good posture ─────────────────────────────
        import pandas as pd
        from walker_domain import FEATURES as WFEATURES
        sample = pd.DataFrame([{
            "height": 1.25, "pitch": 0.05, "pitch_rate": 0.0,
            "thigh": -0.5, "leg": -0.5, "foot": 0.0,
            "thigh_left": -0.5, "leg_left": -0.5, "foot_left": 0.0,
        }])
        d = candidate.mw_distance(sample[WFEATURES])
        val = float(d[0])
        if not (val == val):  # NaN check
            raise ValueError("mw_distance returned NaN on sample")
        DET = candidate
        DET_PATH = p
        print(f"loaded MW detector from {p}, sample distance = {val:.3f}")
        break
    except Exception as e:
        import traceback
        print(f"could not load/validate {p}: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        traceback.print_exc()
        continue

if DET is None:
    print("[MW] WARNING: no usable detector - mw_distances will be empty")
    print("[MW] train one with: ./run_walker_demo.sh --train-detector")

if not POLICIES:
    raise SystemExit("No policies found. Run ./run_demo_walker.sh first.")

DOMAIN_EQ = [
    "joint limits: thigh/leg ∈ [-2.618, 0], foot ∈ [-0.785, 0.785]",
    "reachability: |Δqpos| per step bounded by physics",
    "MW distance: deviation from the trained standing/walking manifold",
]


@app.route("/")
def index():
    return send_from_directory(app.static_folder, "index.html")


@app.route("/api/walker/policies")
def list_policies():
    return jsonify({
        "available": list(POLICIES.keys()),
        "mw_active": DET is not None,
    })


@app.route("/api/walker/run", methods=["POST"])
def run():
    body = request.get_json(silent=True) or {}
    policy = body.get("policy", "balance")
    theta = POLICIES.get(policy)
    if theta is None:
        return jsonify({"ok": False, "error": f"no policy '{policy}'"}), 404

    result = run_policy_rollout(
        theta,
        max_steps=int(body.get("max_steps", 1000)),
        push_at=int(body.get("push_at", -1)),
        push=float(body.get("push", 0.0)),
        det=DET,
        seed=int(body.get("seed", 0)),
    )
    return jsonify({
        **result,
        "policy": policy,
        "domain": "walker2d",
        "equations": DOMAIN_EQ,
    })


@app.route("/api/walker/diagnostics")
def diagnostics():
    return jsonify({
        "policies_loaded": list(POLICIES.keys()),
        "detector_loaded": DET is not None,
        "detector_path": DET_PATH,
        "detector_feature_names": getattr(DET, "feature_names_", None) if DET else None,
        "detector_calibration": {
            "threshold": DET.calibration_.threshold,
            "sigma": DET.calibration_.sigma,
        } if DET else None,
    })


if __name__ == "__main__":
    app.run(host="127.0.0.1", port=5051, debug=False)


# examples/                                                             

===== examples/00_flexible_robot_validator.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
07 - Flexible-link robot: MW as a physics validator for a beam/robot solver
============================================================================
Train MW once on states from an exact Euler-Bernoulli beam solution (a
flexible-link robot arm, or soft-robot segment, released from a deflection
and decaying under damping). Then hand it the output of a cheap approximate
simulator and ask: "how physical is this?" - with no reference trajectory
at hand.

The approximate simulator is dialled by a fidelity knob (0 = mode-2 dropped
entirely plus a mistuned frequency, 1 = exact). Its error should grow as
fidelity falls. A good validator's risk should grow with it too.

SCOPE: this checks whether a GIVEN state is physical. It does not
predict the NEXT state from the current one - see ROBOTICS.md for what a
forward-time forecaster (the Isaac-Sim-adjacent claim) would still need.

Run:  python examples/00_flexible_robot_validator.py   (a few minutes on CPU, faster on GPU)
"""

from _common import args, banner, step, table, takeaway, save, HAS_PLT

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import torch
from mw_framework import MWDetector, roc_auc
from mw_framework.datasets import cantilever_beam, cantilever_beam_coarse, ROBOT_FEATURES
from mw_framework.domains import FlexibleRobotVAE

a = args(__doc__)
banner("07  FLEXIBLE-LINK ROBOT: A PHYSICS VALIDATOR", """
WHY a validator? A robot's compliant links and soft actuators are governed
by a PDE (Euler-Bernoulli beam), not the simple rigid-body ODEs most of a
robot's joints obey. Checking a cheap simulator's output against a fine
PDE solve costs the very compute the cheap simulator was meant to save.

MW learns the manifold of physical beam states once. After that, validating
a state is one encoder-decoder pass: O(n) in the number of features, no
mesh, no mode integration. The laws (moment-curvature constitutive law,
energy-decay envelope, strain limit) are written in FlexibleRobotVAE.
""")

normal, anom = cantilever_beam(n_normal=5000, n_per_anomaly=200, seed=0)
train, test = normal.iloc[:4000], normal.iloc[4000:]
types = anom.anomaly_type.values
ep = 40 if a.quick else 200

step("Train on exact beam-PDE states")
det = MWDetector(vae=FlexibleRobotVAE(), epochs=ep, lr=2e-3).fit(train[ROBOT_FEATURES])
print(f"  false alarms on unseen exact states: {(det.predict(test) == -1).mean():.1%}")

step("Sanity check: labelled faults")
dn, da = det.mw_distance(test), det.mw_distance(anom)
table([[k, f"{roc_auc(dn, da[types == k]):.3f}"] for k in np.unique(types)],
      ["fault", "ROC-AUC"])

step("Validate a coarse simulator at decreasing fidelity")
base = np.median(det.mw_distance(test))
rows, fids, ratios = [], [1.0, 0.8, 0.6, 0.4, 0.2, 0.0], []
for f in fids:
    out = cantilever_beam_coarse(1000, fidelity=f)
    ratio = np.median(det.mw_distance(out[ROBOT_FEATURES])) / base
    top = det.feature_attribution(out[ROBOT_FEATURES]).mean().idxmax()
    gap = np.abs(out.M - out.w_xx).mean()
    rows.append([f"{f:.1f}", f"{gap:.3f}", f"{ratio:.2f}x",
                f"{(det.predict(out[ROBOT_FEATURES]) == -1).mean():.0%}", top])
    ratios.append(ratio)
table(rows, ["fidelity", "mean |M - w_xx|", "MW distance vs exact", "flagged", "top driver"])
print("""
  Read it in three parts:
  - Fidelity 1.0 (exact, 2 modes): the simulator IS the training distribution.
    Distance sits near the exact baseline, as it should.
  - Falling fidelity: mode 2 is dropped and the surviving mode's frequency
    is mistuned. |M - w_xx| alone barely moves (M and w_xx here are built
    from the SAME degraded field, so the constitutive law still holds) -
    the manifold catches the degradation anyway, from the shape of the
    whole trajectory, not from any single law.
  - Flags start at the ~1% false-alarm floor set on held-out exact states.""")

step("Does the LEARNED manifold itself obey the laws?  (2000 generated states)")
v = det.vae_
with torch.no_grad():
    gen = v.to_physical(v.decode(torch.randn(2000, v.latent_dim)))
res = FlexibleRobotVAE.residuals(gen)
print(f"  mean |moment-curvature gap| of generated states: {res['moment_curvature_gap'].abs().mean():.4f}")
print(f"  mean |strain| of generated states:                {res['strain'].abs().mean():.4f}  (limit {9.0})")
print("  (sensor noise in the training data is 0.01 - generated states are as physical as the data)")

if HAS_PLT:
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(5.5, 3.6))
    ax.plot(fids, ratios, "o-")
    ax.axhline(1, color="k", ls="--", lw=1)
    ax.set_xlabel("simulator fidelity (1 = exact)"); ax.set_ylabel("median MW distance / exact")
    ax.set_title("Validator distance tracks simulator error"); ax.grid(alpha=0.3)
    ax.invert_xaxis()
    save(fig, "00_flexible_robot_validator.png")

takeaway("""FIRST EXPERIMENT ON A NEW DOMAIN - strong in parts, with a clear path forward.
The mechanism runs end to end on a genuine robotics PDE: it trains, it scores states in one
pass, and the generated manifold obeys the moment-curvature law and the strain limit - all
with zero changes to the core framework. The no_decay fault (lost damping) is caught
perfectly (AUC 1.00). stiffness_loss and moment_glitch are only partly separated so far
(AUC ~0.57-0.64), and the coarse-simulator sweep moved the wrong way: MW distance FELL as
fidelity dropped, instead of rising as it did for the Navier-Stokes validator in example 06.
Diagnosed, not a mystery: dropping mode 2 removes high-frequency energy content, which this
VAE reads as "closer to a quiet, late-time, low-energy normal state" rather than as "wrong
dynamics" - the laws as written don't yet penalise a physically smoother-than-true
trajectory. That's a specific, fixable target for the next version of these laws (e.g. a
direct modal-content check, or a different feature set), not a dead end. Unchanged from
example 06: this validates a GIVEN state; turning it into a forecaster that predicts the
next state is the natural next step - see ROBOTICS.md.""")

===== examples/01_joint_actuator_validator.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
01 - Robot joint actuator: a torque-vs-dynamics consistency check
===================================================================
A single PD-controlled robot joint (motor + link), the damped second-order
ODE every joint controller's inner loop assumes. Train on the exact step
response; check whether a torque sensor, an encoder and an independent
dynamics model agree, and whether three realistic actuator faults break
that agreement.

Run:  python examples/01_joint_actuator_validator.py   (a couple of minutes)
"""

from _common import args, banner, step, table, takeaway

import numpy as np
import torch
from mw_framework import MWDetector, roc_auc
from mw_framework.datasets import joint_actuator, JOINT_FEATURES
from mw_framework.domains import JointActuatorVAE

a = args(__doc__)
banner("01  ROBOT JOINT ACTUATOR: A TORQUE-VS-DYNAMICS CHECK", """
A robot joint usually has two independent ways to know its own torque: a
current-sense estimate, and what Newton's second law implies from the
encoder's angle, velocity and acceleration. They should agree. MW learns
the manifold of states where they do, then scores any new reading in one
pass - useful for catching bearing wear, gear slip or a failing torque
sensor before it shows up as a control problem.
""")

normal, anom = joint_actuator(n_normal=5000, n_per_anomaly=200, seed=0)
train, test = normal.iloc[:4000], normal.iloc[4000:]
types = anom.anomaly_type.values
ep = 40 if a.quick else 200

step("Train on the exact step-response dynamics")
det = MWDetector(vae=JointActuatorVAE(), epochs=ep, lr=2e-3).fit(train[JOINT_FEATURES])
print(f"  false alarms on unseen exact states: {(det.predict(test) == -1).mean():.1%}")

step("Labelled faults")
dn, da = det.mw_distance(test), det.mw_distance(anom)
table([[k, f"{roc_auc(dn, da[types == k]):.3f}"] for k in np.unique(types)],
      ["fault", "ROC-AUC"])

step("Does the LEARNED manifold itself obey the laws?  (2000 generated states)")
v = det.vae_
with torch.no_grad():
    gen = v.to_physical(v.decode(torch.randn(2000, v.latent_dim)))
res = JointActuatorVAE.residuals(gen)
print(f"  mean |Newton's-law gap| of generated states: {res['newton_gap'].abs().mean():.4f}")
print(f"  mean |torque| of generated states:            {res['torque'].mean():.4f}  (limit 2.0)")
print("  (sensor noise in the training data is 0.01 - generated states are as physical as the data)")

takeaway("""The MW mechanism carries over cleanly to this joint, with no changes to the core
framework. See the fault table above for what a torque/dynamics consistency check catches on
this run - this is an early result from a new domain, not a finished product (see ROBOTICS.md
for the whole robotics picture, what's strong, and what the next iteration should target).""")

===== examples/02_planar_arm_validator.py =====
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
